[PATCH v2 2/8] KVM: arm64: Reset SP_EL1, ELR_EL1 and SPSR_EL1 when
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

Resetting a vcpu keeps the old values of SP_EL1, ELR_EL1 and SPSR_EL1.
Commit e47c2055c68e ("KVM: arm64: Make struct kvm_regs userspace-only")
changed the reset to only clear the general purpose registers, SP, PC
and PSTATE, and later commits moved these three registers to the system
register array without a reset value.  Commit 85d703746154 ("KVM: arm64:
Fully zero the vcpu state on reset") fixed the FP registers and the
32-bit SPSRs the same commit missed, but not these three.

The impact is that the guest that boots next can read the previous
kernel's stack pointer and exception return address.  KVM_GET_ONE_REG
also returns the old values to userspace, and a rebooted VM doesn't
start in the same state as a new one.

Zero them in kvm_reset_vcpu().

Fixes: e47c2055c68e ("KVM: arm64: Make struct kvm_regs userspace-only")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/reset.c | 3 +++
 1 file changed, 3 insertions(+)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH v2 1/8] KVM: arm64: Drop an incomplete MMIO access when
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

When a vcpu exits to userspace for an MMIO access, the next KVM_RUN
completes it.  So for a read, the next run increments the PC counter
and copies data from userspace to the destination vcpu register.

A reset should cancel the MMIO read/write access.  Otherwise the next
run will copy memory from userspace to the vcpu and increment the PC
which skips the first instruction.

Clear vcpu->mmio_needed in kvm_reset_vcpu().

Fixes: 45e96ea6b369 ("KVM: ARM: Handle I/O aborts")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
---
v2: new patch
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/reset.c | 1 +
 1 file changed, 1 insertion(+)

[PATCH v2 0/8] KVM: arm64: Fix kvm_reset_vcpu() and SError
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

Our formal proof work at Pereq found a way to trigger a WARN() by
injecting two SErrors.  Then Sashiko found several related bugs
and testing uncovered a few more.   The original patch is the same,
it's just that I have added others as well.

I have included a selftest for kvm_reset_vcpu().  I have a selftest
for SError as well but I have a question about that so I'm going to
send it seperately later.

[PATCH 2/2] KVM: selftests: Test that an NMI-window single-step preserves the guest's RF
Author: Peng Hao <flyingpenghao@gmail.com>

Add a test for the case where KVM single-steps the IRET of an NMI
handler to find out when NMIs are unblocked (SVM without vNMI) and that
IRET returns to an instruction breakpoint with RF set in the popped
RFLAGS.

The guest arms an instruction breakpoint, sends itself an NMI that is
delivered at the breakpoint address, and from the NMI handler sends a
second NMI, which stays pending, and sets RF in the interrupt frame.
The second NMI is delivered once the first handler's IRET completes,
and when it returns the breakpoint must not fire.

Without the preceding fix the test fails on AMD with kvm_amd.vnmi=0:

  x86/nmi_iret_rf_test.c:80: READ_ONCE(db_count) == 0
  0x1 != 0x0 (READ_ONCE(db_count) != 0)

It passes with vNMI, and on Intel, where KVM doesn't single-step to
open the NMI window.

Signed-off-by: Peng Hao <flyingpeng@tencent.com>
---
 tools/testing/selftests/kvm/Makefile.kvm      |   1 +
 .../selftests/kvm/x86/nmi_iret_rf_test.c      | 109 ++++++++++++++++++
 2 files changed, 110 insertions(+)

[PATCH 1/2] KVM: SVM: Don't clobber RFLAGS.RF loaded by a single-stepped IRET
Author: Peng Hao <flyingpenghao@gmail.com>

Without vNMI, KVM opens the NMI window after an NMI handler by
intercepting IRET and then single-stepping it with TF and RF set.  When
the single-step #DB arrives, disable_nmi_singlestep() clears TF and RF
again unless they were set in the RFLAGS snapshot taken when the
single-step was armed, i.e. before the IRET ran.

That is wrong for RF.  By the time the single-step trap is taken, the
stepped instruction has completed and the CPU has already cleared the RF
that KVM set, so RF in save.rflags is guest state.  It is only set if
the instruction loaded it, which is what the stepped IRET does when the
NMI interrupted a context that was about to resume from an instruction
breakpoint: the OS sets RF in the saved RFLAGS so the breakpoint is not
hit again, the NMI pushes that RFLAGS and the NMI handler's IRET pops
it.  KVM then clears RF based on the stale snapshot, and the guest takes
a second #DB on the same instruction.

In a Linux guest this shows up as a duplicate perf hardware breakpoint
event when a PMI happens to land right after the breakpoint has been
handled, e.g. while sampling with perf at a high rate.

If the single-step trap fired, fold the post-instruction RF into the
snapshot so that disable_nmi_singlestep() leaves it alone.  RF is still
cleared on the event injection path in svm_vcpu_run(), where the
instruction never ran and RF still holds KVM's value.

TF has the same problem in principle when the stepped IRET pops TF=1,
but there KVM cannot tell its own TF apart from the guest's after the
fact, so it is left as is.

Fixes: 44c11430b52c ("KVM: inject NMI after IRET from a previous NMI, not before.")
Signed-off-by: Peng Hao <flyingpeng@tencent.com>
---
 arch/x86/kvm/svm/svm.c | 12 ++++++++++++
 1 file changed, 12 insertions(+)

[PATCH v4 4/5] KVM: x86: nVMX: move nested APICv state to nested_guest_state
Author: Maxim Levitsky <mlevitsk@redhat.com>

Replace vmx->nested.pi_pending,pi_active and posted_intr_nv with
KVM_NESTED_PI_VECTOR_MASK,KVM_NESTED_PI_ACTIVE,KVM_NESTED_PI_PENDING
flags of nested_guest_state.

Since the nested state is cleared as a whole to 0, on each nested VM exit,
explicit clearing of vmx->nested.posted_intr_nv and vmx->nested.pi_pending
was dropped.

Also, since nested entry sets nested_guest_state to KVM_NESTED_IN_GUEST,
the publishing of KVM_NESTED_PI_VECTOR_MASK,KVM_NESTED_PI_ACTIVE is delayed
to happen after enter_guest_mode().

Suggested-by: Paolo Bonzini <pbonzini@redhat.com>
Signed-off-by: Maxim Levitsky <mlevitsk@redhat.com>
---

[PATCH v4 5/5] KVM: x86: nVMX: fix races in delivery of nested posted interrupts
Author: Maxim Levitsky <mlevitsk@redhat.com>

If a nested posted interrupt races with a L0 vm exit on a target vCPU,
that is reflected to L1, then this nestedf posted interrupt is currently
neither processed nor delivered to L1 as a regular interrupt.

Since it is not possible to know if APICv hardware has processed
the interrupt, opt to always process it a moment before nested VM exit.
(call vmx_complete_nested_posted_interrupt)

To ensure that vmx_deliver_nested_posted_interrupt and
vmx_complete_nested_posted_interrupt don't race do the following:

Have a cmpxchg based loop in vmx_deliver_nested_posted_interrupt that
atomically checks that target vCPU is in nested mode and has suitable
posted interrupt configuration, and then set KVM_NESTED_PI_PENDING.

If the above cmpxchg loop succeeds, this means that even if the target vCPU
is in process of exiting the nested guest, it will notice this
KVM_NESTED_PI_PENDING flag when it reaches its
vmx_complete_nested_posted_interrupt, and then it will process it.

If it fails, it means that the target vCPU is no longer in the guest
mode, and thus it is safe to deliver the interrupt as a normal interrupt.

Apply a similar logic on the receiving side:
Use xchg to snapshot the old value of guest state in leave_guest_mode,
and by using this snapshot, determine if we need to process
the posted interrupts.

Also delay publish to of the nested posted interrupt state to
KVM_REQ_GET_NESTED_STATE_PAGES to avoid dealing with a situation where
vm exit causes a L2->L2 vm exit before nested APICv pages were mapped.

Fixes: 705699a13994 ("KVM: nVMX: Enable nested posted interrupt processing")

Suggested-by: Paolo Bonzini <pbonzini@redhat.com>
Signed-off-by: Maxim Levitsky <mlevitsk@redhat.com>
---

 arch/x86/kvm/vmx/nested.c | 63 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++-------------
 arch/x86/kvm/vmx/vmx.c    | 60 ++++++++++++++++++-------------------
 3 files changed, 76 insertions(+), 52 deletions(-)

[PATCH v4 1/5] KVM: nVMX: don't check PID.ON when processing nested posted interrupts
Author: Maxim Levitsky <mlevitsk@redhat.com>

Despite the suggestion to test and set the PID.ON and avoid sending
a posted interrupt if it is already set, there is no requirement
for this bit to be set for posted interrupt processing to happen
when the target CPU receives the posted notification vector.

See section 30.6 POSTED-INTERRUPT PROCESSING:

   "The processor clears the outstanding-notification bit in
    the posted-interrupt descriptor. This is done atomically so as to leave
    the remainder of the descriptor unmodified
    (e.g., with a locked AND operation)."

Apparently Windows' implementation of APICv does exactly this:
it sets bits in PIR, without bothering to also set the PID.ON.

Fixes: 705699a13994 ("KVM: nVMX: Enable nested posted interrupt processing")

Signed-off-by: Maxim Levitsky <mlevitsk@redhat.com>
---
 arch/x86/kvm/vmx/nested.c | 15 +++++++++++----
 1 file changed, 11 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)

[PATCH v4 0/5] KVM: nVMX: fix nested APICv emulation for windows guests
Author: Maxim Levitsky <mlevitsk@redhat.com>

Fix two problems that were recently discovered that cause nested APICv=0D
to malfunction on windows with hyperv enabled.=0D
=0D
The first problem is that unlike KVM, windows doesn't set PIR.ON when=0D
sending posted interrupts. X86 spec actually allows this.=0D
=0D
The second problem is that unlilke KVM, which mostly ignores them,=0D
windows depends on accurate delivery of the posted notification=0D
interrupts, and currently it is possible for a posted notification=0D
to get lost it it arrives at the same time as the nested VM exit.=0D
=0D
V2: to avoid spurious notification in L1, process the posted=0D
notification in L2, just before VM exit.=0D
=0D
V3: rework the patch series following the pseudocode that Paolo suggested=0D
(Thanks!!)=0D
=0D
V4: random fixes, including sashiko feedback.=0D
=0D
Best regards,=0D
	Maxim Levitsky=0D
=0D

  KVM: x86: split vcpu->arch.hflags into nested_guest_state and smm_mode=0D

  KVM: x86: nVMX: move nested APICv state to nested_guest_state=0D

[PATCH] KVM: arm64: GICv3: Keep active LPIs in the LRs
Author: Brad Fitzpatrick via B4 Relay <devnull+bradfitz.tailscale.com@kernel.org>


Since commit 73c9726975af ("KVM: arm64: GICv3: Drop LPI active state
when folding LRs"), the active state of a virtual LPI is discarded on
every exit. If the guest has acknowledged an LPI but not yet EOI'd it
when an exit happens, the LPI is then pruned from the ap_list, and the
guest's subsequent EOI targets an INTID that is not in any LR. KVM then
relies on the virtual CPU interface performing the priority drop
regardless.

That doesn't hold for Apple's emulated GICv3 interface, as exposed by
macOS Virtualization.framework to a Linux guest running with nested
virtualization (M3 and later, e.g. "tart run --nested"). There, an EOI
of a vLPI that has no LR leaves the corresponding ICH_AP1R0_EL2 bit set.
The running priority of the vCPU stays at the LPI's priority forever,
and since Linux uses the same priority for all of its interrupts,
nothing is ever delivered to that vCPU again. The nested guest's CPU
spins on WFI, with KVM seeing pending interrupts and waking it up over
and over (about 30k WFI exits per second), while the guest itself
reports a hard lockup on that CPU. With a 2-vCPU Debian guest, this
happens within the first minute of boot, every time.

The architecture is arguably ambiguous here: the ICV_EOIR1_EL1
description says that a write "drops the priority for the virtual
interrupt" and the VirtualWriteEOIR1() comment says "just priority drop
and return", but the pseudocode itself only drops the priority on that
path if the IMPLEMENTATION DEFINED "Drop before checks" behaviour is
implemented (Arm IHI 0069E, 9.20 Pseudocode).

Avoid depending on this when we don't have to: keep the active state of
LPIs when folding the LRs, so that an acknowledged LPI stays in its LR
until the guest EOIs it, as was the case before v6.19. The only LPIs
whose active state is now dropped are the ones that don't make it into
the LRs, which is where EOIcount can't track their deactivation. On
implementations like Apple's, that case still relies on the priority
drop, but it now requires LR overflow instead of a single exit at the
wrong time.

Tested on Apple M5 Pro, M4 Pro and M1 Macs and on AWS Graviton3.

Fixes: 73c9726975af ("KVM: arm64: GICv3: Drop LPI active state when folding LRs")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Brad Fitzpatrick <bradfitz@tailscale.com>
---
This is a bug report in the form of a vibe-coded patch, which came out of
me asking Claude to bisect why my Linux kernel upgrade broke nested virt
on my Mac mini Proxmox cluster. Things worked fine with
proxmox-kernel-6.17 (6.17.13-1-pve), but every kernel option after that
(7.0.14-30-pve, 7.3.5-1-pve, mainline 7.4-rc4) was busted: Linux guests
of the Proxmox node hang during boot with a hard lockup on one vCPU.

Claude bisected it and tested this patch, which makes Proxmox nested virt
work on my Mac minis again (both M5 Pro and M4 Pro minis).

Claude slop text follows. I don't claim to know any of this. You're
welcome to ignore this patch entirely and fix it in a proper way.

The setup: macOS's Virtualization.framework (via Tart, "tart run
--nested") runs an arm64 Linux VM with EL2, which runs KVM in nVHE mode
as a Proxmox node; that node runs a Linux guest. The vGIC in the middle
VM is Apple's emulated GICv3 CPU interface (ICH_VTR_EL2 = 0x90580007,
8 LRs, 5 priority bits, TDS set).

What goes wrong, per instrumentation of the middle kernel: the stuck vCPU
has ICH_AP1R0_EL2 = BIT(24), i.e. an active priority of 0xc0, with no
interrupt active in KVM's view, and all four used LRs still holding
pending interrupts (timer PPI 27, SGIs 1 and 6, an LPI). Since Linux uses
0xc0 for all of its interrupts, nothing is ever delivered again; the vCPU
spins on WFI about 30k times per second with KVM waking it every time.
Logging every time vgic_v3_fold_lr() discards an LPI's active state shows
that it happened exactly once in a hung run, on the vCPU that then got
stuck, for virtio-blk's MSI at priority 0xc0.

So the guest's EOI of an LPI that is no longer in any LR doesn't drop
the priority on Apple's emulated GIC. The patch keeps active LPIs in the
LRs, as before v6.19, and drops the active state only of LPIs that don't
fit in the LRs. Details are in the commit message.

This looks specific to Virtualization.framework's emulated GIC, not to
Apple silicon: on an M1 MacBook Pro running Asahi Linux on bare metal,
with Fedora Asahi Remix's stock 7.3.4 kernel (which includes
73c9726975af1), the same Debian sid guest under QEMU/KVM, using the ITS
and LPIs, booted fine 4/4 times with 2 and 4 vCPUs. So M1's hardware vGIC
does the priority drop.

Testing:

  - Nested (KVM in nVHE mode in a Tart VM), QEMU guest (Debian sid, 2
    and 4 vCPUs), M5 Pro Mac mini on macOS 27.0: unpatched 7.4-rc4+ hung
    5/5 boots, patched booted 4/4.
  - Same on an M4 Pro Mac mini on macOS 26.6.2: unpatched hung 2/2,
    patched booted 4/4.
  - Nested, real Proxmox VE 9.2 node on the M5 Pro, booting a Proxmox
    guest (Debian sid): 6.12 and 6.17.13-1-pve boot, 7.0.14-30-pve hangs,
    and patched 7.4-rc4+ built with Proxmox's full config booted 4/4 (2
    and 4 vCPUs), with the node's cluster and Ceph services healthy.
  - Bare metal M1 (Asahi, Fedora's 7.3.4-401.asahi.fc43 source): boots
    4/4 both unpatched, as above, and with this patch applied on top.
  - Bare metal AWS Graviton3 (c7g.metal, Neoverse-V1, GICv3 with ITS, 4
    LRs), mainline 4dce97c6c265 with and without this patch, each in VHE,
    nVHE and protected mode:
      - KVM selftests: identical results with and without the patch in
        every mode (VHE and nVHE: 40 pass, 9 skip, 0 fail; protected: 37
        pass, 10 skip, 2 fail, where vgic_init gets EINVAL instead of
        E2BIG and mmu_stress_test times out, both without the patch too).
      - vgic_lpi_stress at up to 16 vCPUs and 64 devices: passes.
      - Debian sid guests with 2, 4 and 16 vCPUs and 32 virtio-blk
        queues under O_DIRECT read load: boot and finish, no stalls.
      - To exercise the LR overflow path with active LPIs: a local copy
        of vgic_lpi_stress whose guest handler spins between ack and EOI,
        with a kprobe on vgic_v3_configure_hcr() histogramming the
        ap_list summary on every guest entry. With this patch, the LRs
        overflowed while LPIs were active 904 times (VHE), 376 (nVHE) and
        344 (protected), and all 160 runs passed. Without the patch that
        count is always 0, since LPI active state is dropped on exit.

Not tested: kvm-unit-tests, GICv4.1 direct injection (KVM reported
"GICv4 support disabled" on the Graviton3), and any non-Apple, non-AWS
hardware.

[PATCH v2 20/20] KVM: arm64: Advertise ECV, HAFT and ETS3 to protected VMs
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

capped at IMP and HAFDBS at HAFT, since CNTPOFF and HDBSS only add EL2
and EL3 controls. ECV's self-synchronised counter views trap under the

[PATCH v2 11/20] KVM: arm64: Compute a protected vCPU's fine-grained traps at EL2
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

pKVM sets a protected vCPU's HCR_EL2, MDCR_EL2 and HCRX_EL2 at EL2 from
the VM's ID registers, but never computes its fine-grained traps, which
stay 0. That traps every register with a negative-polarity trap bit, so
a feature such as S1PIE can't be advertised to a protected VM.

Link config.c into nVHE hyp and compute the VM's FGU masks and the
vCPU's fine-grained traps there, under vm_table_lock, once the ID
registers are set. Build hyp without the latent_entropy plugin, which
adds a reference to config.c's __init functions that hyp can't resolve.

This also restores the AmpereOne AC03_CPU_38 masking of a protected
vCPU's TCR_EL1 writes, which was lost when the erratum's trap moved
into the per-vCPU fine-grained traps.

Fixes: fb10ddf35c1cc ("KVM: arm64: Compute per-vCPU FGTs at vcpu_load()")
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---

[PATCH v2 10/20] KVM: arm64: Advertise the GICv3 CPU interface to protected VMs on GICv5
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

A GICv5 host with FEAT_GCIE_LEGACY runs GICv3 guests, but its own
ID_AA64PFR0_EL1.GIC is 0. KVM sets the field to IMP for non-protected
VMs on such a host, but not for protected VMs, which read it from EL2's
copy of the register. The next patch computes a protected vCPU's
fine-grained traps from its ID registers, and with GIC at 0, EL2 would
then inject an UNDEF for the guest's ICC_IGRPEN<n>_EL1 accesses.

Set the field to IMP in EL2's copy as well. The vgic isn't probed yet
when the copy is made, so test the capability the vgic itself tests.

Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

[PATCH v2 08/20] KVM: arm64: Move the VM's FGU initialisation into config.c
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

The first time one of a VM's vCPUs runs, kvm_calculate_traps() computes
the VM's fine-grained UNDEF masks from its ID registers, one
compute_fgu() call per FGT group, and sets KVM_ARCH_FLAG_FGU_INITIALIZED
so that it computes them once. The next patch computes the same masks
for protected VMs at EL2.

Move the calls and the flag into kvm_init_fgu() in config.c, so that a
group added later is computed at EL2 too. compute_fgu() then has no
caller outside config.c, so make it static.

No functional change intended.

Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

[PATCH v2 07/20] KVM: arm64: Allow protected VMs to use SVE
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Now that EL2 owns a protected vCPU's SVE state and advertises
ID_AA64ZFR0_EL1, allow KVM_CAP_ARM_SVE for a protected VM, which lets
KVM_ARM_VCPU_INIT take KVM_ARM_VCPU_SVE.

A protected guest's SVE access trap injects an UNDEF. An SVE
instruction takes the SVE trap rather than the FP one, so a guest whose
first vector instruction is an SVE one would get an UNDEF instead of
the lazy FP switch. Handle the SVE trap as the FP trap is:
kvm_handle_pvm_fpsimd() switches the state for a vCPU with SVE and
still injects the UNDEF for one without.

Update the documentation to match.

Tested-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
Reviewed-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

[PATCH v2 06/20] KVM: arm64: Advertise ID_AA64ZFR0_EL1 to protected VMs
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

EL2 builds a protected guest's ID registers from the host's sanitised
values, most of them capped per field. It has no case for
ID_AA64ZFR0_EL1, so the register reads as 0, and a protected guest with
SVE wouldn't see any of the SVE extensions the CPUs implement.

Keep a copy of the host's sanitised ID_AA64ZFR0_EL1 at EL2, and
advertise it when the guest has SVE, as KVM does for a non-protected
guest. Cap each field at the highest value the sysreg file defines.

Tested-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
Reviewed-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

[PATCH v2 05/20] KVM: arm64: Allocate a protected vCPU's SVE state at EL2
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

For a vCPU with SVE, EL2 pins the buffer the host allocated at
KVM_ARM_VCPU_FINALIZE and saves the guest's SVE registers there. The
host can still read and write that buffer, so it can't hold a protected
vCPU's state.

Allocate a protected vCPU's SVE state from the hyp heap instead, and
free it with the vCPU. When EL2 resets a protected vCPU, zero its SVE
state and ZCR_EL1, as KVM does for a non-protected one. The host still
allocates its own buffer, which KVM_{GET,SET}_ONE_REG access before the
first run, but EL2 doesn't use it.

For a protected vCPU, the host's sve_max_vl now sizes an EL2 allocation
and is the guest's maximum vector length on every CPU. Give EL2 a copy
of kvm_sve_max_vl, the largest length all CPUs can give a guest, and
check every vCPU's sve_max_vl against it rather than clamping it to the
host's own maximum. set_sve_vls() already keeps the host's value within
that bound.

Tested-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
Reviewed-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>

[PATCH v2 04/20] KVM: arm64: Remove redundant kern_hyp_va() calls from the pKVM FP sync
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

With pKVM, EL2 saves FP and SVE state through the hyp vCPU, whose kvm
is the hyp VM's and whose sve_state is already a hyp address.
kern_hyp_va() returns both unchanged.

Remove the calls in fpsimd_sve_sync() and __hyp_sve_save_guest(), and
the one in kvm_hyp_save_fpsimd_host(), which only pKVM reaches. The
ones in __hyp_sve_restore_guest() and kvm_hyp_handle_fpsimd() stay, as
nVHE without pKVM reaches them with the host's pointers.

No functional change intended.

Tested-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
Reviewed-by: Vincent Donnefort <vdonnefort@google.com>
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/include/hyp/switch.h | 2 +-
 arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/nvhe/hyp-main.c      | 4 ++--
 2 files changed, 3 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-)

[PATCH v2 03/20] KVM: arm64: Add support for self-hosted debug in protected guests
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

pKVM hides DebugVer from protected guests and makes their debug
registers RAZ/WI or UNDEFINED, so they don't have breakpoints,
watchpoints or single-step. For a non-protected guest, the host sets the
owner of the debug registers at vcpu load, from the guest's MDSCR_EL1
and OS Lock, and EL2 copies the owner and the registers in at each
entry. However, the host can't see a protected guest's registers.

Set the owner at EL2 for a protected vCPU instead, by the rules the host
uses for a non-protected guest, but keep MDCR_EL2.TDA set when the guest
is the owner. A trap handler returns to the guest without going back
through the world switch, so a handler that changes the owner switches
the debug registers itself. The PSCI CPU_ON reset runs after the vcpu
load, so it sets the owner again.

Advertise DebugVer, capped at V8P8 as for a non-protected guest, and the
breakpoint, watchpoint and context-aware breakpoint counts. The other
debug registers that took an UNDEF behave as they do for a non-protected
guest that doesn't own its debug registers.

Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---
 Documentation/virt/kvm/arm/pkvm.rst    |   7 +-
 arch/arm64/include/asm/kvm_hyp.h       |   1 +
 arch/arm64/kvm/arm.c                   |   1 +
 arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/include/nvhe/pkvm.h |   1 +
 arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/nvhe/hyp-main.c     |   2 +

[PATCH v2 02/20] KVM: arm64: Don't advertise the host's MTE_frac to protected VMs
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Protected VMs don't support MTE, so MTE_frac in their ID_AA64PFR1_EL1
doesn't apply and is RES0, as it is for a non-protected VM without MTE.

Commit 3d7ff00700d1a ("KVM: arm64: Rework specifying restricted
features for protected VMs") caps the field at NI in the pVM
restriction table. However, NI is 0xF and the clamp is unsigned, so the
cap does nothing and a protected guest sees the host's value. On a CPU
with MTE2 but without FEAT_MTE_ASYNC, that value is 0xF.

Drop the entry so that the field reads as 0 again. It was the only
entry capping an unsigned field at an NI other than 0.

Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/hyp/nvhe/sys_regs.c | 1 -
 1 file changed, 1 deletion(-)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH v2 01/20] KVM: arm64: Only allow ptrauth for a pVM on a system with full ptrauth
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

EL2 allows a protected VM both ptrauth features whatever the system
supports. An id_aa64isar1 override can leave a system with generic but
not address auth. EL2 then doesn't trap the guest's key accesses, but
it doesn't switch the keys either, since the switch is gated on
ARM64_HAS_ADDRESS_AUTH. The host can read and write the guest's
APGAKey.

Only allow the ptrauth features when the system has full ptrauth, as
the host does.

Fixes: b56680de9c648 ("KVM: arm64: Initialize trap register values in hyp in pKVM")
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---
 arch/arm64/include/asm/kvm_pkvm.h | 11 +++++++----
 1 file changed, 7 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH v2 00/20] KVM: arm64: Support debug, SVE, MOPS, PIE/POE, FPMR and more in protected VMs
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Hi folks,

Changes since v1 [1]:
  - Replaced the #ifdef around the feature map check with a WARN_ON().
    (Oliver)
  - Added two fixes, for ptrauth and MTE_frac.
  - Added a patch advertising GICv3 to protected VMs on GICv5 hosts with
    legacy support.
  - Small fixes and tidying up.
  - Rebased on 7.4-rc3.

With most of the groundwork merged, it's time to bring protected VMs up
to the features we support for them in Android.

  01-02:  Fixes for ptrauth and MTE_frac.
  03:     Self-hosted debug.
  04-07:  SVE, with EL2 owning a protected vCPU's SVE state.
  08-11:  Compute a protected vCPU's fine-grained traps at EL2.
  12-20:  Advertise TCR2, SCTLR2, S1PIE, S1POE, MOPS, FPMR and the
          features EL2 already handles for non-protected guests.

Tested on QEMU, in hVHE and nVHE, and on FVP for S1POE, GICv5 in legacy
mode and the fields QEMU doesn't model (CLRBHB, RPRFM, ECBHB, LSFE,
LSUI, HAFT, ETS3 and F16MM). v2 of the pKVM selftests series [2] covers
debug, SVE, MOPS, PIE, POE and FPMR.

Based on Linux 7.4-rc3 (aebb727447640).

Cheers,
/fuad

[2] https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261109084854.840576-1-fuad.tabba@linux.dev/

[PATCH] KVM: arm64: selftests: Pin vpmu_counter_access to one CPU
Author: James Clark <james.clark@linaro.org>

The vpmu_counter_access test reads the maximum PMCR_EL0.N from one VM
and then creates new VMs for each number of counters up to that limit.
If the test migrates between CPU types the limit may not be valid for
the new VM which causes:

  ==== Test Assertion Failure ====
    arm64/vpmu_counter_access.c:483: !ret
    pid=9165 tid=9165 errno=22 - Invalid argument
  ...
    KVM_SET_DEVICE_ATTR failed, rc: -1 errno: 22 (Invalid argument)

For example on a Radxa Orion O6 with 20 counters on the big cores and 6
on the little cores, this happens on most runs.

Fix it by pinning the test to the CPU that it starts on before any VMs
are created.

Signed-off-by: James Clark <james.clark@linaro.org>
---
Assisted by Claude Opus 5.5.
---
 tools/testing/selftests/kvm/arm64/vpmu_counter_access.c | 3 +++
 1 file changed, 3 insertions(+)

is unavailable, but returns without closing it if setting the address
or initialising the ITS fails. Route all errors after device creation
through the same close path.

[PATCH kvmtool v6 00/23] arm64: Support GICv5-based guests
Author: Sascha Bischoff <sascha.bischoff@arm.com>

This series adds support for GICv5-based VMs to kvmtool. These are
exposed as --irqchip=gicv5 and --irqchip=gicv5-its, where the former
creates a vGICv5 device and the latter adds a GICv5 ITS. The GICv5
specification can be found at [1].

The GICv5 support in KVM needed by --irqchip=gicv5 is available as of
Linux v7.4-rc1. The GICv5 ITS support is described in [2]; using
--irqchip=gicv5-its requires a KVM build with those changes.

The series teaches kvmtool to create the GICv5 irqchip and ITS KVM
devices. It describes the GICv5 CPU interface, IRS and ITS in the
guest DT, updates the timer and PMU interrupt descriptions, and
generates GICv5 interrupt specifiers for platform devices and PCI. It
also maps PCI MSI requester IDs to the ITS. The IRS support is built
and exposed before the ITS support, so the GICv5 irqchip support can
be merged separately from the ITS changes.

Compared with v5 [3]:

* Dropped the SETLPI frame from the IRS DT description because KVM
  reserves but does not emulate it. Updated patch 15's commit message
  accordingly.

* Added Vladimir's Reviewed-by trailers to patches 9 and 15.

The v7.4-rc1 header sync includes GICv5 IRS UAPI, but not GICv5 ITS
UAPI. Patch 19 mirrors the ITS definitions from [2]. Once that UAPI
lands upstream, the headers should be resynced against the latest
Linux headers before merging the ITS support.

Thanks,
Sascha

[1] https://developer.arm.com/documentation/aes0070/latest
[2] https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261102194454.4086911-1-sascha.bischoff@arm.com/
[3] https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261118175755.1946977-1-sascha.bischoff@arm.com/

[PATCH] KVM: arm64: Drop a pending MMIO access when injecting an
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

Oliver's commit bfb7a30b1986 ("KVM: arm64: Don't retire MMIO instruction
w/ pending (emulated) SError") established the rule that when userspace
injects an SError that we drop the MMIO.  An MMIO access dropped this way
runs again when the guest returns from the SError.

However, since commit 3bd107ed6b23 ("KVM: arm64: Only emulate an SError's
entry for vCPUs with NV"), a vcpu without EL2 pends the SError through
HCR_EL2.VSE instead.  The next KVM_RUN completes the access, and then
the guest takes the SError.  So the behavior is not consistent depending
on whether the vcpu has EL2 (MMIO dropped) or not (MMIO completes).

To make things consistent, drop the pending MMIO access in the non-EL2
case as well.

This change only affects unmasked SErrors, if they are masked the MMIO
always completes.  Protected vcpus are left alone, because the host's
copy of their PSTATE can't be trusted.

I discovered this inconsistency while writing a selftest and I don't
believe real life users have noticed it.

Fixes: 3bd107ed6b23 ("KVM: arm64: Only emulate an SError's entry for vCPUs with NV")
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
---
I think this patch is correct but it's also easy enough to modify my
selftest to accept the current behavior.
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/inject_fault.c | 8 ++++++++
 1 file changed, 8 insertions(+)

[PATCH v2 8/8] KVM: arm64: selftests: Test that a vcpu reset
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

Add a test to check that KVM_ARM_VCPU_INIT (which resets a vcpu) has
reset all the things it was supposed to.

The test dirties as much of the vcpu state as it can, resets the vcpu,
and compares every register in KVM_GET_REG_LIST with a freshly
initialised vcpu.  The are a few things which we must not clear such as
MPIDR_EL1, which userspace configures and the reset keeps, and the
CLIDR_EL1 and CCSIDR counters which keep counting.

The test runs with and without EL2.

Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

[PATCH v2 7/8] KVM: arm64: nv: Re-inject a stored SError after the
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

KVM only has room for one pending exception or PC increment per vcpu.
If something is already pending and we try to add another through
kvm_pend_exception() or kvm_incr_pc(), it triggers a WARN().  If we add
an exception on top of an exception, the first one is lost.  If we add a
PC increment on top of an exception, the PC increment is lost and the
trapped instruction runs again.

Normally an exit handler records an exception or a PC increment, and
__kvm_adjust_pc() commits it before the next guest entry.  However, with
nested virtualization kvm_nested_sync_hwstate() can add an exception
before the exit handler runs.  It re-injects a stored SError after the
guest exits but before handle_exit(), which can pend the SError as an
exception.  The exit handler then records its own on top, for example an
HVC forwarded to the guest hypervisor, the FPAC for a failed ERETAA, or
the PC increment for a trapped instruction it has emulated.  This
triggers the WARN().

In this situation where we have two exceptions, the correct order is to
handle the exit first and then the SError.  Change the
kvm_nested_sync_hwstate() function to only store the SError.  Then
kvm_nested_flush_hwstate() uses __kvm_adjust_pc() to commit the exit
handler's exception or PC increment, re-injects the SError, and the
guest is entered.  The early re-injection was there so that a pending
SError wakes the vcpu from WFI, so kvm_arch_vcpu_runnable() now treats
a deliverable stored SError as a wakeup.

Fixes: 77ee70a07357 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Honor SError exception routing / masking")
Fixes: ce66109cec86 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Take "masked" aborts to EL2 when HCRX_EL2.TMEA is set")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
---
v2: No change
---
 arch/arm64/include/asm/kvm_emulate.h |  1 +
 arch/arm64/kvm/arm.c                 |  1 +
 arch/arm64/kvm/inject_fault.c        |  6 ++++++
 arch/arm64/kvm/nested.c              | 16 ++++++----------
 4 files changed, 14 insertions(+), 10 deletions(-)

[PATCH v2 6/8] KVM: arm64: nv: Don't deliver a pending SError to
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

With nested virtualization, when we inject an SError that the guest
has masked, we set the VSE bit in ->hcr_el2 and the CPU delivers it once
the guest unmasks SErrors.  The problem is that the bit stays set when
we switch between L1 and L2, so an SError meant for L2 can be delivered
to L1, and the other way around.

When we switch, clear the VSE bit and set NESTED_SERROR_PENDING instead.

This matches what kvm_nested_sync_hwstate() does after every guest exit.
Then kvm_nested_flush_hwstate() injects the SError again and decides
where it should go in the new context.

Reported-by: Sashiko <sashiko-bot@kernel.org>
Closes: https://lore.kernel.org/all/sashiko-outbox-229065@kernel.org/
Fixes: 77ee70a07357 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Honor SError exception routing / masking")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
---
v2: new patch
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/emulate-nested.c | 14 ++++++++++++++
 1 file changed, 14 insertions(+)

[PATCH v2 5/8] KVM: arm64: nv: Replace a pending SError when
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

The kvm_inject_serror_esr() function injects SErrors as the name
implies.  The bug is that it doesn't handle already pending errors
correctly in nested virtualization.

In vanilla KVM the kvm_inject_serror_esr() function always sets
the SError to pending.  However with nested KVM, it might set it
to pending or it might deliver the SError directly.  We can only
have one pending error at a time.  Imagine we enter the function
with an SError pending already.  In the current code if we decide
to pend the new error that's fine, everything works.  However if
we decide to deliver it directly then the pend flags should be
cleared but they are not.  So we deliver the SError directly and
then the SError that was pending on the next exit or entry.

Clear the pend flags unconditionally at the start of the function.

Reported-by: Sashiko <sashiko-bot@kernel.org>
Closes: https://lore.kernel.org/all/sashiko-outbox-229065@kernel.org/
Fixes: ce66109cec86 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Take "masked" aborts to EL2 when HCRX_EL2.TMEA is set")
Fixes: 77ee70a07357 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Honor SError exception routing / masking")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
---
v2: New patch
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/inject_fault.c | 4 ++++
 1 file changed, 4 insertions(+)

[PATCH v2 4/8] KVM: arm64: nv: Discard nested SError in
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

Vanilla KVM stores a pending SError in ->hcr_el2 but nested KVM stores
it in the NESTED_SERROR_PENDING flag.  Clear the flag for nested
SErrors when we reset the vcpu.

[PATCH v2 3/8] KVM: arm64: Discard a pending SError when resetting
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

Resetting a vcpu with the KVM_ARM_VCPU_INIT ioctl keeps a pending
SError.  We used to rely on vcpu_reset_hcr() to clear the SError but
commit f1ff3fc5209a ("KVM: arm64: unify code to prepare traps") changed
it to only reset HCR_EL2 before the vcpu's very first KVM_RUN.

If we reboot a guest while there is a pending SError then KVM delivers
it to the freshly booted guest.

Clear HCR_EL2.VSE in kvm_reset_vcpu().

Fixes: f1ff3fc5209a ("KVM: arm64: unify code to prepare traps")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

[PATCH v2 2/8] KVM: arm64: Reset SP_EL1, ELR_EL1 and SPSR_EL1 when
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

Resetting a vcpu keeps the old values of SP_EL1, ELR_EL1 and SPSR_EL1.
Commit e47c2055c68e ("KVM: arm64: Make struct kvm_regs userspace-only")
changed the reset to only clear the general purpose registers, SP, PC
and PSTATE, and later commits moved these three registers to the system
register array without a reset value.  Commit 85d703746154 ("KVM: arm64:
Fully zero the vcpu state on reset") fixed the FP registers and the
32-bit SPSRs the same commit missed, but not these three.

The impact is that the guest that boots next can read the previous
kernel's stack pointer and exception return address.  KVM_GET_ONE_REG
also returns the old values to userspace, and a rebooted VM doesn't
start in the same state as a new one.

Zero them in kvm_reset_vcpu().

Fixes: e47c2055c68e ("KVM: arm64: Make struct kvm_regs userspace-only")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/reset.c | 3 +++
 1 file changed, 3 insertions(+)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH v2 1/8] KVM: arm64: Drop an incomplete MMIO access when
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

When a vcpu exits to userspace for an MMIO access, the next KVM_RUN
completes it.  So for a read, the next run increments the PC counter
and copies data from userspace to the destination vcpu register.

A reset should cancel the MMIO read/write access.  Otherwise the next
run will copy memory from userspace to the vcpu and increment the PC
which skips the first instruction.

Clear vcpu->mmio_needed in kvm_reset_vcpu().

Fixes: 45e96ea6b369 ("KVM: ARM: Handle I/O aborts")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
---
v2: new patch
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/reset.c | 1 +
 1 file changed, 1 insertion(+)

[RFC PATCH v2 26/30] tools/unwind: Add standalone libLLVM Minimal
Author: Ian Rogers <irogers@google.com>

Add an optional standalone host verification tool (tools/unwind/verify_cfi,
built via `make -C tools/unwind` or `make tools/unwind` when libLLVM is
detected via tools/build/Makefile.feature, and gracefully skipped
otherwise) linked directly against kernel/unwind/dwarf_cfi.c.

This initial commit implements the CLI harness, host VMA and kmem buffer
wrappers (dwarf_cfi_host.c), and Oracle 1 (verifyOracle1Dwarf()), which
evaluates libLLVM's full reference DWARFUnwindTable
(llvm::dwarf::createUnwindTable) and verifies exact agreement with both
dwarf_cfi_unwind_user_eh_frame_hdr() and dwarf_cfi_unwind_eh_frame_hdr()
at every FDE start, row-transition boundary (pc - 1 and pc), and end PC.

Across 207,450 vmlinux FDEs (2,575,276 transition PCs) on x86_64, arm64,
and riscv64, plus user-space shared libraries (libc.so.6, libstdc++.so.6,
libcrypto.so.3, /bin/bash), Oracle 1 reports 0 mismatches.

[RFC PATCH v2 21/30] scripts: Add eh_frame_min.py .eh_frame coverage
Author: Ian Rogers <irogers@google.com>

Add an optional standalone developer inspection script
(scripts/eh_frame_min.py, not invoked by Kbuild) to analyze ELF32/ELF64
relocatable objects (.o, .ko), vmlinux images, user-space executables,
and shared libraries across x86_64, i386, arm64, arm, and riscv.

It reports .text / .eh_frame / .eh_frame_hdr / .sframe / .orc_unwind
section sizes, simulated minified .eh_frame savings, Minimal DWARF FDE
coverage (and breakdown of any unsupported opcodes), kept SP/FP/RA vs.
ignored GPR register rules, maximum DW_CFA_remember_state depth, and top
DWARF CFA opcode counts.

Example usage:
  $ ./scripts/eh_frame_min.py vmlinux
  $ ./scripts/eh_frame_min.py mm/vmscan.o kernel/fork.o
  $ ./scripts/eh_frame_min.py /bin/bash /lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libc.so.6
  $ ./scripts/eh_frame_min.py --minify /tmp/eh_frame.min mm/vmscan.o

Signed-off-by: Ian Rogers <irogers@google.com>

Author: Ian Rogers <irogers@google.com>

callchain unwinding (PERF_RECORD_CALLCHAIN_DEFERRED) compiled with

[RFC PATCH v2 01/30] unwind: Add Minimal DWARF CFI bytecode interpreter
Author: Ian Rogers <irogers@google.com>

Add a strictly bounded DWARF Call Frame Information (.eh_frame /
.eh_frame_hdr) interpreter (CONFIG_UNWIND_DWARF_CFI) matching SFrame's
stack-unwinding state model and security properties, without requiring
new toolchain sections or duplicating user-space unwind metadata.

Instead of implementing a general DWARF expression virtual machine or
tracking arbitrary architectural register arrays, struct dwarf_cfi_row
tracks only:
 - CFA = (SP | FP | prologue scratch reg) + cfa_off, or fixed-pattern
   indirect CFA *(FP + off) (DRAP stack realignment) / *(SP + off)
   (signal trampolines)
 - Stack pointer save rule (SAME or *(CFA + off)) for signal frames that
   save SP in sigcontext (such as 32-bit x86 vdso32 __kernel_rt_sigreturn)
 - Frame pointer save rule (SAME, *(CFA + off), *(FP + off), *(SP + off))
 - Return address save rule (SAME in link register, *(CFA + off),
   *(SP + off), or UNDEFINED for bottom-of-stack termination), plus
   AArch64 PAC-RET signing state (DW_CFA_AARCH64_negate_ra_state)

Callee-saved general-purpose register spill/restore opcodes are decoded
and ignored in O(1) space. General DW_OP_* expressions, DW_CFA_set_loc
backward PC jumps, and DW_CFA_register rules on {SP, FP, RA} are
rejected with -EINVAL; DW_EH_PE_indirect encodings are rejected whenever
an encoded address is evaluated (while unused 'P'/'L' CIE augmentation
fields are skipped by encoded width without dereferencing). CIEs
(<= 128 B), FDEs (<= 4096 B), LEB128 operands (<= 5 B), and
DW_CFA_remember_state nesting (<= 4 entries) execute in a single forward
pass with zero dynamic memory allocations, zero locks, KMSAN output
unpoisoning, noinstr kernel-memory entry points, and Spectre v1 / v1.1
nospec hardening (array_index_nospec() and barrier_nospec()).

Also provide shared RCU-safe loadable-module .eh_frame indexing helpers
using kvmalloc()/kvfree_rcu_mightsleep() (dwarf_cfi_mod_alloc(),
dwarf_cfi_mod_free_rcu(), dwarf_cfi_mod_eval()).

Signed-off-by: Ian Rogers <irogers@google.com>
---
 arch/Kconfig                     |    6 +
 arch/arm64/Kconfig               |    3 -
 include/linux/unwind_dwarf_cfi.h |  181 +++
 kernel/unwind/Makefile           |   12 +-
 kernel/unwind/dwarf_cfi.c        | 1923 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 5 files changed, 2121 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[RFC PATCH v2 03/30] unwind_user: Support Minimal DWARF CFI
Author: Ian Rogers <irogers@google.com>

Extend the generic user-space unwinder (kernel/unwind/user.c) with
UNWIND_USER_TYPE_CFI (CONFIG_HAVE_UNWIND_USER_CFI), resolving ELF
PT_GNU_EH_FRAME headers across executable VMAs in kernel/unwind/user_cfi.c.

Key design details:
 - Find the executable VMA and its base ELF mapping (vm_pgoff == 0)
   under mmap_read_lock_killable(), then drop mmap_read_lock() before
   issuing any faultable copy_from_user() reads.
 - Cache VMA -> PT_GNU_EH_FRAME lookups (including negative lookups for
   anonymous/JIT VMAs so unwind_user_next() falls back per frame to FP)
   in a 4-slot per-walk cache on struct unwind_user_state.
 - Track stack-pointer validity (state->sp_valid and
   unwind_user_fp_sets_sp()) across mixed CFI -> FP -> CFI walks so
   architectures whose FP step does not recover exact caller SP reject
   SP-based rules until an outer FP-based FDE or sigreturn restores SP,
   and support signal-frame SP recovery from *(CFA + sp_off).
 - Provide overridable unwind_user_frame_pointer() (guarded by
   CONFIG_HAVE_UNWIND_USER_FP), unwind_user_fp_sets_sp(), and
   unwind_user_sigreturn() hooks alongside unwind_user_link_register()
   and unwind_user_strip_pac(), and allow up to UNWIND_USER_MAX_SIGNALS
   signal frames per walk to switch stacks (SA_ONSTACK).
 - Support link-register leaf frames, DRAP/sigframe indirect CFA rules,
   and clean bottom-of-stack termination on DW_CFA_undefined or zero RA.

Signed-off-by: Ian Rogers <irogers@google.com>
